\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q}^{-}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\underline{Q}_{-}}(\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q}^{-})_{\nu}
.
Under the identification
(\underline{Q})_{-}\simeq(Q_{-}^{\sigma})^{\prime}\subset Q_{-}
,
Theorem 2.6 implies that
we have a weight space decomposition

\mathbf{V}_{q}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in(Q^{\sigma}_{-})^{\prime}}(\mathbf{V}_{q})_{\nu}
, and

\Phi
induces an isomorphism
{}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}(\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q})_{\nu}\,\raise 2.0pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\sim}$}\,(\mathbf{V}_{q})_{\nu}

for each
\nu\in\underline{Q}_{-}
.
Note that
\{\pi(b)\mid b\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}\}
gives a signed basis of
\mathbf{V}_{q}
. Hence

\{\pi(b)\mid b\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}(\nu)\}
gives a signed basis of
(\mathbf{V}_{q})_{\nu}
,
where
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}(\nu)=\{b\in\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}\mid\nu(b)=\nu\}
.
In particular, we have

(6.22.1)

|\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}(\nu)|/2=\dim(\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q}^{-})_{\nu}\quad\text{ for }\quad\nu\in(Q^{\sigma}_{-})^{\prime}\simeq\underline{Q}_{-}.

6.23.

We consider the modified PBW-basis

\mathscr{X}^{\natural}_{\prec}=\{L^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec)\mid\mathbf{c}\in\mathscr{C}\}

for the case where
p=0
.
Note that it is not verified that
\sigma
leaves the set
\mathscr{X}^{\natural}_{\prec}
invariant.
By 6.4,
S^{\prec}_{\mathbf{c}_{0}}=T_{w}S_{\mathbf{c}_{0}}
, and
w
is the minimal lift of

\underline{w}=\prod_{i\in\eta_{0}-\{0\}}s_{i}
. Recall that
\mathbf{c}_{0}=(\rho^{(i)})_{i\in I_{0}}

is an
I_{0}
-tuple of partitions. Let
\mathscr{C}_{0}
be the set of
I_{0}^{\prime}
-tuple of partitions,
and we regard
\mathbf{c}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}_{0}
as an
I_{0}
-tuple of partition such that

\rho^{(i)}=\emptyset
for
i\notin I_{0}^{\prime}
.
It follows that
S_{\mathbf{c}_{0}}^{\prec}=S_{\mathbf{c}_{0}}
for
\mathbf{c}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}_{0}
.

\sigma
acts naturally on
\mathscr{C}_{0}
by
\sigma(\rho^{(i)})=\rho^{\sigma(i)}
, and
we have
\sigma(S_{\mathbf{c}_{0}})=S_{\sigma(\mathbf{c}_{0})}
for
\mathbf{c}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}_{0}
. We denote by

\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}_{0}
the set of
\sigma
-fixed elements in
\mathscr{C}_{0}
.
Let
\mathbf{c}_{+}=(c_{\beta})_{\beta\prec\delta},\mathbf{c}_{-}=(c_{\gamma})_{\gamma\succ\delta}
be as in 6.4.
Let
\mathscr{C}_{+}
be the set of
\mathbf{c}_{+}
such that
c_{\beta}=0
for
\beta\in\varDelta^{(1)}_{>}
,
and
\mathscr{C}_{-}
the set of
\mathbf{c}_{-}
such that
c_{\gamma}=0
for
\gamma\in\varDelta^{(1)}_{<}
.
We denote by
\mathscr{C}_{\pm}^{\sigma}
the set of
\sigma
-fixed elements in
\mathscr{C}_{\pm}
.
Let
\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}
be the set of triples
\mathbf{c}=(\mathbf{c}_{+},\mathbf{c}_{0},\mathbf{c}_{-})
such that

\mathbf{c}_{\pm}\in\mathscr{C}_{\pm}^{\sigma},\mathbf{c}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}_{0}^{\sigma}
. The set of triples

\underline{\mathscr{C}}=\{\underline{\mathbf{c}}=(\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{;},\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{0},\underline{\mathbf{c}}_{+})
, and the set of
PBW-basis
\underline{\mathscr{X}}_{\underline{\mathbf{h}}}=\{L(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0)\mid\underline{\mathbf{c}}\in\underline{\mathscr{C}}\}

are defined as in [BN]. As in [SZ2], one can check that we have a natural
bijection
\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}\simeq\underline{\mathscr{C}}
.
Let
(\mathscr{X}^{\natural}_{\prec})^{\sigma}
be the set of
\sigma
-fixed PBW-bases
contained in
\mathscr{X}^{\natural}_{\prec}
. The following result was proved
in [SZ2, Theorem 6.13] in the case where
\sigma
is admissible with
\varepsilon\neq 4
.
We can prove it in the general case by using (6.22.1) in a similar way.

Theorem 6.24
.

Assume that
\sigma
is admissible with
\varepsilon=4
, or not admissible satisfying the condition
in Theorem 2.6. Then

(i)

(\mathscr{X}^{\natural}_{\prec})^{\sigma}=\{L^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec)\mid\mathbf{c}\in\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}\}
.

(ii)

\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}=\pm\{b^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec)\mid\mathbf{c}\in\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}\}
.

6.25.

We denote by
\underline{\mathbf{c}}\mapsto\mathbf{c}
the bijection
\underline{\mathscr{C}}\,\raise 2.0pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\sim}$}\,\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}
.
For each
\mathbf{c}\in\mathscr{C}^{\sigma}
,
L^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec)
gives an element in

{}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}_{q}^{-,\sigma}
, hence
\pi(L^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec))\in\mathbf{V}_{q}
. On the other
hand, for each
\underline{\mathbf{c}}\in\underline{\mathscr{C}}
, one can consider
L(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0)\in\underline{\mathscr{X}}_{\underline{\mathbf{h}}}

and its image
\Phi(L(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0))\in\mathbf{V}_{q}
. We have the following result.

Theorem 6.26
.

Let
\Phi:{}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q}^{-}\,\raise 2.0pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\sim}$}\,\mathbf{V}_{q}
be the isomorphism given in
Theorem 1.4 or Theorem 2.6. In particular
\underline{X}
is not of type

A_{2}^{(2)}
nor
A_{1}^{(1)}
. Then

(i)

\Phi(L(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0))=\pi(L^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec))
for each
\underline{\mathbf{c}}\in\underline{\mathscr{C}}
.

(ii)

\Phi(b(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0))=\pi(b^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec))
for each
\underline{\mathbf{c}}\in\underline{\mathscr{C}}
.

(iii)

The natural map

\textstyle{{}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}_{q}^{-,\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \pi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{V}_{q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{-1}}
\textstyle{{}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{q}^{-}}

induces a bijection
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\sigma}\,\raise 2.0pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\sim}$}\,\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{B}}}
,
which is explicitly given by
b^{\natural}(\mathbf{c},\prec)\mapsto b(\underline{\mathbf{c}},0)
.

Proof.

Recall that
\underline{\mathbf{h}}=(\cdots,\eta_{-1},\eta_{0},\eta_{1},\dots)
is the doubly infinite
sequence given in 6.1. In order to prove (i), it is enough to see that

(6.26.1)

\displaystyle\Phi(\underline{T}_{\eta_{0}}\underline{T}_{\eta_{-1}}\cdots\underline{T}_{\eta_{k+1}}(\underline{f}_{\eta_{k}}))

\displaystyle=\pi(\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{0}}\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{-1}}\cdots\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{k+1}}(\widetilde{f}_{\eta_{k}})),

\displaystyle\text{ if $k\leq 0$, }

\displaystyle\Phi(\underline{T}_{\eta_{1}}^{-1}\underline{T}_{\eta_{2}}^{-1}\cdots\underline{T}_{\eta_{k-1}}^{-1}(\underline{f}_{\eta_{k}}))

\displaystyle=\pi(\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{1}}^{-1}\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{2}}^{-1}\cdots\widetilde{T}_{\eta_{k-1}}^{-1}(\widetilde{f}_{\eta_{k}})),

\displaystyle\text{ if $k\geq 1$. }.

This is reduced to the case where the rank of
\underline{X}
is 2. As we have
excluded the case where
\underline{X}
is of type
A_{2}^{(2)}
or
A_{1}^{(1)}
,
\underline{X}

is of finite type of rank 2. We have verified (6.26.1) in [SZ1]
in the case where
\sigma
is admissible with
\varepsilon\neq 4
. Thus the remaining one is the case
where
(X,\underline{X})
is of type
(A_{4},C_{2})
, which was already verified in
the proof of Theorem 3.7.
Hence (6.26.1) holds, and (i) follows. (ii) follows from (i), and
(iii) follows from (ii). The theorem is proved.
∎
